1 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
 
 
 
 
STAFF REPORT 
 
 
To:   Snyderville Basin Planning Commission   
From:   Amir Caus, Senior Planner and Ray Milliner, Principal Planner 
Date of Meeting: March 26, 2024 
Type of Item:  MPD-Rezone, MPD -Conditional Use Permit, MPD-Low Impact Permit,  
   Development Agreement, and Final Subdivision Plat – Work Session 
Process:  Legislative and Administrative Review 
 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:  The applicant is requesting a Master Planned Development for 
the Marketplace at Silver Creek, a 10-building mixed use project located at 1500 Frontage 
Road (Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM), Silver Summit, Summit County, UT.   
 
Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission review the proposed 
Marketplace at Silver Creek Master Planned Development (MPD) and provide feedback and 
direction on the proposed request. 
 
This is an introductory work session. No formal action is requested at this meeting.  A 
public hearing will be scheduled in the future.  
 
The applicant has stated that their specific objectives of this meeting include; 
 
- “Provide a preliminary introduction to the Planning Commission prior to formal 
application to include development massing models, subdivision plat, definitive 
work force and market housing AMI allocation strategy. 
- Determination of suitability of site and accompanying massing and density. 
- Determination of entitlement methodology; split zones between CC and NMU. 
- Determination of appropriateness of building heights. 
- Confirmation of density per UE’s and AUE’s. 
- Affirmation of workforce obligation using entitlement methodology subject to final 
exhibit. 
- Affirmation of parking reduction based on transportation initiatives, structured-
parking and final shared parking plan.” 
 
The applicant has included building plans, elevations, and section drawings in the 
proposal package to discuss height appropriateness and to demonstrate how the buildings 
relate to natural grade.  
 
4
2 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
Project Description 
 
Project Name: Marketplace at Silver Creek MPD 
Applicant(s):   Henry Sigg 
Property Owner(s):  Marketplace at Silver Creek Center LLC 
Location:   1500 Frontage Road, Silver Summit, Summit County, UT  
Zone District:   Community Commercial (CC) 
Parcel Number and Size: SCO-C-10-3AM (14.76 acres) 
Type of Process:  Legislative (Rezone and Development Agreement)-  
    Administrative (Conditional Use Permit, Low Impact Permit,  
    and Final Subdivision Plat) 
Final Land Use Authority: Summit County Council (Rezone and Development   
    Agreement) and Snyderville Basin Planning Commission  
    ( Conditional Use Permit, Low Impact Permit, and Final   
    Subdivision Plat) 
 
Vicinity Map 
 
 
 
Proposal 
 
The applicant is requesting a Master Planned Development for the Marketplace at Silver 
Creek, a 10-building mixed use project located at 1500 Frontage Road (Parcel SCO-C-10-
3AM), Silver Summit, Summit County, UT.  Of the 14.76 acres, the applicant is proposing to 
5
3 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
designate ~8 acres under the Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) Zone and the remaining 
~6.7 acres under the existing Community Commercial Zone.  
 
The project includes ~123,000 sq. ft. of grocery, retail, and restaurant space. The project 
also includes 409 residential units of which 235 will be affordable, designated at 80% Area 
Median Income (AMI) or less, and 174 will be designated market rate. The final physical 
unit breakdown and affordable housing obligation will be confirmed once more detailed 
plans have been finalized and submitted, including a determination of the required 
Affordable Unit Equivalents (AUEs). 
 
The applicant has stated that; “The intent is to not have “dark” residences. Leases will be 
mandated at 12 month minimums and units deed restricted from nightly rentals. A live 
community.” The applicant has stated also that; “the unit counts are not final and subject 
to minor change and that ratios of income brackets are still TBD.”  
 
The applicant is proposing a reduction in parking as required by the NMU section of the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code.  
 
Subdivision 
 
The applicant plans on subdividing the property into three lots. Buildings 1 and 2 would be 
located on Lot 1 (~6.7 acres), Buildings 3 and 4 would be located on Lot 2 (~4.7 acres), and 
Buildings 5-10 would be located on Lot 3 (~3.2 acres).  
 
The applications/processes involved in this project include; 
 
• MPD-Rezone to Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) Zone (~8 acres of 14.76 acres) 
• MPD-Conditional Use Permit 
• MPD-Low Impact Permit; 
• Development Agreement 
• Final Subdivision Plat (3 Lots) 
 
Background 
 
Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM (14.76 acres) is located in the Silver Creek Commerce Center 
Subdivision and is currently undeveloped. The Silver Creek Commerce Center Subdivision 
was approved in 1988. The subdivision has been amended several times, culminating in 
the current layout.  
 
Silver Creek Commerce Center Subdivision is not governed by a Consent Agreement or 
Development Agreement.  
 
Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM is located within the Community Commercial (CC) Zone. The 
density in the CC zone is determined by the ability of the proposed development to meet 
all required development and performance standards and criteria set forth in the 
Development Code. 
6
4 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
This project was originally introduced to the Planning Commission as a work session on 
June 12, 2018. At the time, the proposal included: 
 
• Commercial retail, including a ~62,000 square foot building, various smaller retail 
structures and a bank; 
• Restaurant uses, including a coffee shop, a brew pub with roof top seating and 
garden;  
• Office uses, generally within a residential or commercial building; 
• Workforce housing 21 units; 
• Market rate housing, 82 units; 
• Live work units 5 living units over 5 commercial units; 
• Hotel (75 rooms); 
• An amphitheater 
• Underground parking; 
• Public plaza 
 
Comments at the meeting generally revolved around the walkability of the project and the 
impact that it would have on traffic in the immediate area. The Planning Commission 
directed that a traffic study would be appropriate.  
 
Following the 2018 work session, the applicant was informed that the County was 
interested in purchasing the Florence J. Gillmor property immediately south of his Lot. The 
project was put on hold to work with the County on possible land exchanges or 
development opportunities.  
 
In September 2019, Summit County purchased the Florence J. Gillmor property, 
approximately 160 acres immediately to the south of the applicant property. The County 
subdivided it into four lots. On November 7, 2022, the applicant and the County submitted 
a plat amendment to vacate 3.22 acres from parcel FJGILL-1-AM-X (county property) and 
add it to Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM (applicant property). In exchange, the applicant vacated 
0.12 acres from Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM and added it to Parcel FJGILL-1-AM-X. The purpose 
of the exchange was to provide the County with an access to the Gillmor property via the 
East side of Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM. 
 
The County Council also approved a zoning boundary determination to zone the 3.22 acres 
vacated from Parcel FJGILL-1-AM-X and added to Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM to the Community 
Commercial Zone and the 0.12 acres vacated from Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM and added to 
Parcel FJGILL-1-AM-X to the Rural Residential Zone. 
 
Analysis and Findings 
                                                                                                                                                                    
Of the 14.76 acres, the applicant is proposing to designate ~8 acres under the 
Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) Zone and the remaining ~6.7 acres under the existing 
Community Commercial Zone.  
7
5 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
Existing Community Commercial Zone 
 
As part of the overall mixed-use project, the applicant is requesting that Building 1 of 10 be 
designated as a single-story grocery store with an ancillary retail tenant space. Per the 
NMU-1 Zone; “Single-Story Commercial Building Standards: Unless otherwise approved 
through the Master Planned Development process, no single-story commercial structures 
greater than one thousand (1,000) square feet are permitted in the NMU-1 zone.” 
According to the applicant; “The industry standard and requested grocery store building in 
our demographic is one story.” Therefore, the applicant is requesting to use ~6.7 acres of 
existing Community Commercial Zone for the purpose of Building 1 of 10, a 1-story 
~38,000 sq. ft. grocery store with a 1-story ~5,400 of retail tenant space (attached to the 
grocery store).  
 
In addition to Building 1, the applicant is also keeping Building 2 of 10 within the CC Zone. 
Building 2 is a multi-story building with ~20,000 sq. ft. of retail and a ~700 transit center. 
The applicant has stated that they chose to keep Building 2 within the CC Zone because it 
could not be integrated into the proposed structured parking.  
 
All parking proposed at the base zoning maximum allowed (3.5 spaces per 1,000 sq. ft. 
ratio).  
 
MPD-Rezone to Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) Zone 
 
Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM is currently zoned CC where density is determined by the ability of 
the proposed development to meet all required development and performance standards 
and criteria set forth in the Snyderville Basin Development Code. Building height in the CC 
Zone is limited to 32 feet which can limit design opportunities for mixed use developments. 
The NMU-1 Zone was created for the specific purpose of creating new development areas 
that are mixed use, pedestrian-focused, and transit-oriented. Building height in the NMU-1 
Zone is allowed up to 45 feet with an exception up to 60 feet to accommodate affordable 
housing and/or structured parking. The applicant is requesting a Rezone to the NMU-1 
Zone to allow for additional flexibility to accommodate additional affordable housing and 
structured parking.  
 
Future Land Use Map 
 
The East Basin Neighborhood Planning Area Future Land Use Map contained in the General 
Plan identifies Parcel SCO-C-10-3AM as a Mixed Use Area. 
 
Current General Plan Neighborhood Area Description 
 
The subject property is located in the East Basin Neighborhood Planning Area.  
 
The neighborhood description states; “The East Basin neighborhood is highly visible 
from US-40 and others areas in the immediate vicinity. It is an important entry corridor 
into the Snyderville Basin and an additional entrance into Park City.” 
8
6 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
 
The neighborhood description also states; “ The visual connectivity to existing open 
spaces is an important community feature and should be preserved as much as 
possible. Future design objectives that support this goal, such as locating structures at 
the outer edge of the meadow, clustering development near existing uses, and avoiding 
strip mall type patterns of  development are encouraged. Protection of existing wildlife  
corridors is an important  community value. Consideration should be given for future 
mixed-use developments and flexibility in design standards...”  
 
MPD-Conditional Use Permit 
 
The following proposed uses are to be reviewed through the MPD-Conditional Use Permit 
review process; 
 
• Dwelling unit, multi-family 
• Retail sales, larger than 40,000, less than 60,000 square feet in size 
• Offices, intensive 
• Hotel, motel, or inn 
 
MPD-Low Impact Permit 
 
Per Section 10-2-18(B.3) MPD Applicability; “Any new hotel or lodging project, commercial, 
retail, office, public, quasi-public, mixed use, or industrial project with ten thousand 
(10,000) square feet or more of gross floor area.” 
 
The following proposed uses are to be reviewed through the MPD-Low Impact Permit 
review process; 
o Retail sales, general 
o Restaurant, full service 
§ No drive-throughs being proposed 
o Offices, general, moderate, medical and dental 
o Dwelling unit, multi-family 
 
Combination of Processes 
 
Section 10-3-2(C) of the Snyderville Basin Development Code states; “Legislative Actions: 
The director or county council may, in their discretion, combine the processing and hearing 
of development permits, procedures for legislative action, or requests for major or minor 
development review, conditional use permit, or low impact permit. (Ord. 708, 12-10-2008)” 
 
Due to the several processes involved for the proposal, the Community Development 
Director has elected to combine the review process, however each appropriate Land Use 
Authority would take their separate actions. All Final Actions on the MPD CUPs and LIPs 
would need to be conditioned such that they would be subject to Council approval of 
Rezone and any Development Agreement. 
 
 
9
7 
Marketplace at Silver Creek – March 26, 2024 
Service Providers 
 
A service provider review will be requested once more detailed plans have been finalized 
and submitted.  
 
Recommendation 
 
The applicant is seeking Snyderville Basin Planning Commission discussion, feedback, 
and direction on the proposed Marketplace at Silver Creek Master Planned Development 
(MPD). The applicant has stated that their specific objectives of this meeting include; 
 
- “Provide a preliminary introduction to the Planning Commission prior to formal 
application to include development massing models, subdivision plat, definitive 
work force and market housing AMI allocation strategy. 
- Determination of suitability of site and accompanying massing and density. 
- Determination of entitlement methodology; split zones between CC and NMU. 
- Determination of appropriateness of building heights. 
- Confirmation of density per UE’s and AUE’s. 
- Affirmation of workforce obligation using entitlement methodology subject to final 
exhibit. 
- Affirmation of parking reduction based on transportation initiatives, structured-
parking and final shared parking plan.” 
 
No formal action is requested at this meeting.  A public hearing will be scheduled in the 
future. 
 
Public Notice, Meetings and Comments 
  
This item was publicly noticed as a Work Session with the Snyderville Basin Planning 
Commission.   
 
Public hearing notices will be published and courtesy postcards will be mailed for future 
public hearings. 
 
As of the date of this Staff Report, no public comment has been received.

                                           MARKETPLACE AT SILVER CREEK CENTER 
 
 
DESCRIPTION: 
• Approximately 15 acres currently zoned community commercial (CC) at the corner of 
Old Highway 40 and Silver Creek Drive. The General Plan identifies the site as 
compatible for the New Mixed Use Overlay Zone. 
 
 
ENTITLEMENT: 
• The application seeks to entitle the property under the two existing underlying zones; 
Community commercial and New Mixed Use and to subdivide the property into three 
lots of record. One lot containing single level uses including a grocery store and out- 
building processed under Community Commercial Zoning with surface parking. One lot 
containing stacked residential units processed under The New Mixed-Use Zoning with 
structured parking. One lot containing a mix of retail and restaurant uses on two levels 
with stacked residential units above and processed under the New Mixed-Use Zoning 
with both surface and structured parking. The entire development seeks to be 
processed under the same application. 
 
 
PROJECT USES: 
• 409 total residential units. Note; no square foot difference between UE and AUE units. 
• 128 Studio, 102 One Bedroom, 141 Two Bedroom and 38 Three Bedroom units. 
• 334 Total unit equivalents (UE’s) comprised of 91 FTE work force unit equivalents 
(AUE’s) for the commercial obligation and 243 residential unit equivalents comprised of 
162 market UE’s and 81 workforce AUE’s for the NMU residential obligation. 
• The 162 Market unit equivalents targeted for > 80% percent AMI. Either for sale or for 
rent (TBD). The total number of Market units is 174. 
• The 172 affordable unit equivalent work force units targeted for < 80% AMI. The total 
number of affordable units is 235. 
• No nightly rentals. A live project with excellent live/work opportunity. 
• 122,948 total square feet of grocery, service retail and restaurant. 
• 41,000 square foot grocery store (TBD). 
• 61,948 square feet of service/consumer retail (TBD). 
• 20,000 square feet of restaurant space (TBD). 
 
EXHIBIT A.1
11
 
 
 
PARKING: 
• The parking strategy is to implement code reference 10-1-3, New Model For 
              Neighborhood and Community Development by establishing parking scaled for typical  
              use rather than worst case.  
• The NMU Zone 10-2-17 seeks to facilitate community design principles that result in 
               a reduction in the need for parking and that are transit oriented. The development is  
               located along an existing transit system and within 1/4 mile of the HVTA facility. 
• As an MPD the development seeks to modify parking as required in 10-4-9 of the 
               code to reflect the residential occupancy profile and concentrates parking 
               availability based on intensity of use. 
• Implementation of a no minimum parking requirement per the MPD is supported by; 
providing structured parking, providing transit centers, supporting transportation 
initiatives, establishing single household occupancy requirements, standardizing 
UE sizes equally between AUE units and Market units, concentrating parking in areas 
based on intensity of use and through implementation of a shared parking strategy based 
 on off peak and on peak utilization of parking spaces. 
• The development proposes a 27.4 % reduction in code required parking. 
• The calculation of code required parking per 10-4-9 of the code is 1033 spaces. The 
project program provides 750 spaces in a combination of structured and above grade 
spaces. Code required parking totals 1033 spaces. 
• There is a minimum of one parking space for each residential unit and a ratio of 
2.71/1000 (341) square foot of commercial with a 4/1000 square foot area located in 
the immediate proximity of the grocery store. 
• The adjustment to the code required parking established in 10-4-9 is as follows; Studio 
Units - .75 (96) of total unit requirement (128), One-bedroom Units - 1.00  (102) of total 
unit requirement (102), Two Bedroom Units - .75 (141) of total unit requirement (212), 
Three Bedroom Units - .66 (38) of Total unit requirement (76).  The total number of 
residential spaces is 486 leaving a total of 264 spaces assigned to commercial. 
• Guest parking and parking shortfall from section 10-4-9 of the code shall be provided 
per an on peak/off peak allocation of underutilized commercial spaces based on 
business hours of operation (i.e.; available between the hours of 8pm and 8am) 
                 
 
FEATURES: 
• Two, micro pull out transit centers with interior waiting areas containing restrooms. 
• Structured parking with bicycle storage areas for residents. 
• Interior surface vehicular parking with e bike parking areas. 
EXHIBIT A.2
12
 
 
• Streetscape along Silver Creek Drive and perimeter commuter trails. 
• Sustainable site development. Best practices earthwork and project layout. 
• Designed to future R/W benchmarks and proposed roundabout. 
• Meaningful pedestrian and residential gathering areas. 
• Rooftop gathering areas to encourage socio-economic diversity. 
• Safe internal pedestrian and vehicular traffic circulation. 
• Diverse housing options ranging from 50% AMI to Market rate. 
• Potential 55 plus residential building. 
• No nightly rentals. Twelve-month minimum occupancy periods. 
• Close, proximity to vast meaningful open space. 
• Proximity to High Valley Transit Facility. 
• Reduced minimum residential parking to drive transportation initiatives. 
• Designed for responsible, non-intrusive phasing. 
• Multiple access points and circulation to surrounding R/W’s. 
• Proximity to future Summit County civil areas. 
• Visioned and designed to minimize trip generated traffic to other sectors of the basin.

EXHIBIT A.45

10-2-17: NEIGHBORHOOD MIXED-USE 1 (NMU-1) ZONE:
   A.   Purpose:
      1.   The purpose of the 
Neighborhood Mixed-Use 1 (NMU-1) zone is to create new
development and redevelopment areas that are:
         a.   Mixed use;
         b.   Pedestrian-focused; and
         c.   Transit-oriented.
      2.   The desired development pattern of the NMU-1 zone shall be facilitated through flexible site
and community design principles that:
         a.   Result in a mix of land uses that are compatible, mutually supportive, well connected
through a variety of transportation choices;
         b.   Result in convenient, safe, direct, and well-connected transit and pedestrian linkages;
         c.   Result in a reduction in the need for parking;
         d.   Result in compact development that creates public spaces, including but not limited to
plazas and other outdoor amenity areas; and
         e.   Result in sustainable infrastructure and building design consistent with best practices.
   B.   Uses: Uses in the NMU-1 zone are limited to the following:
      1.   Allowed Uses:
         a.   Bars, taverns, clubs;
         b.   Home based business, Class 1 (see Section 10-8-4);
         c.   Mixed use structure;
         d.   Mobile food business;
         e.   Open space;
         f.   Park, community;
         g.   Park, neighborhood;
         h.   Recycling facilities, Class 1;
         i.   Solar array, minor;
         j.   Telecommunication facilities, co-location;
         k.   Trails, community wide;
         l.   Trails, neighborhood;
         m.   Trailhead parking;
         n.   Offices, general; and
         o.   Retail sales, convenience store.

   C.   Rezoning Eligibility: Properties must meet all the following criteria to be eligible for
consideration for a rezone to IMMU-1:
      1.   The property(s) must be designated for mixed-use on the Snyderville Basin General Plan,
Future Land Use Map;
      2.   The property(s) must be adjacent to (or a redevelopment of) existing commercial, mixed-
use, or institutional/civic development:
      3.   The property(s) must be located along an existing transit system or within the County
Master Planned Development process, receive a written commitment to provide service from the
County Regional Transportation Planning Director.
      4.   Property subject to the terms and conditions of any prior development agreement,
settlement agreement or similar instrument shall remain subject to those terms and conditions until
such time as the prior agreement is amended or terminated as part of the Master Planned
Development Process.

EXHIBIT B.3
58
   D.   Rezoning And Master Planned Development: A proposal for a rezone to the NMU-1 zone
shall be processed concurrently with a master planned development application subject to this title.
   E.   Density: The maximum density in the NMU-1 shall be determined by the ability of the
proposed development to meet all required development and performance standards and criteria
set forth in this title.
   F.   Setbacks:
      1.   Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development process, setbacks in
the NMU-1 zone shall be as follows:
Exterior Boundary Setback
To mitigate negative impacts, preserve view corridors or
create a compatible street design/streetscape, the
planning commission or county council may modify the
setbacks around the exterior boundary of the project. In
some cases, that setback may be increased to create an
adequate buffer to adjacent uses at the discretion of the
county. The planning commission/county council may
reduce setbacks within the project from those otherwise
required provided the project meets minimum
International Building Code and Fire Code requirements
and advances the goals set forth in the General Plan.
Front Setback
The minimum front setback is twenty feet (20') for all
structures. The twenty foot (20') front setback may be
reduced to ten feet (10'), provided all on-site parking is at
the rear of the property or is in structured parking.
Front Setback (if property line
extends to the centerline of a road)
The minimum front setback is forty-five feet (45') for all
structures. The forty-five foot (45') front setback may be
reduced to twenty feet (20'), provided all on-site parking is
at the rear of the property or is in structured parking. All
structures and improvements, excluding driveways, are to
be fully contained on the lot.
Side Setback The minimum side setback is ten feet (10').
Rear Setback The minimum rear setback is ten feet (10').
Wetland Setback Forty feet (40') from delineation line as defined by the
Army Corps of Engineers.
East Canyon Creek Setback One hundred fifty feet (150') from centerline.
River or Perennial Stream Setback One hundred feet (100') from centerline.
Lake or Natural Pond Setback Fifty feet (50') from high water mark.
Designated Roadway Setback,
Highways 224, 40, 248, and
Interstate 80
One hundred feet (100') from the edge of the right of way.
Frontage Road Setback, including,
but not limited to, Kilby Road,
Rasmussen Road, Bitner Road,
North Pace Frontage Road, and
US-40 Frontage Road
Sixty feet (60') from the edge of the right of way.

EXHIBIT B.4
59
      2.   On a corner lot, one minimum front setback and one minimum side setback is required with
the following provisions:
         a.   Any frontage with a driveway leading to a garage or parking space shall have a front
setback.
         b.   On any corner lot, a clear view area must be maintained. This is a triangular area formed
by the property lines abutting the street and a line connecting them at points twenty-five feet (25')
from the intersection of the property lines. No obstruction to view more than three (3) feet in height
shall be placed in the clear view area, including walls, fences, structures, signs, trees, shrubs, or
hedges. When topography presents a clear view, the area shall be graded to provide visual
clearance.
   G.   Height: Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development (MPD)
process, the maximum building height in the NMU-1 zone shall be forty-five feet (45').
   Exception: The county council may grant a height exception up to a maximum of sixty feet (60') to
accommodate affordable housing and/or structured parking through the MPD process.
   For any building sharing a common property line with a detached single-family dwelling unit, the
maximum height is limited to forty-five feet (45') and may not be increased through any process.
   Step Back Requirement: Floors rising above thirty-five feet (35') in height shall be stepped back
fifteen (15) horizontal feet from the building foundation at grade for building elevations that are
facing a public street, public trail, or public open space. This step back does not apply to balconies
on buildings with floors rising above thirty-five feet (35') in height.
   H.   Performance Standards: In addition to compliance with all Master Planned Development
criteria set forth in this title, the following performance criteria shall apply to the NMU-1 zone.
      1.   Open Space: Unless otherwise approved through the Master Planned Development
process, all Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone shall provide twenty-five percent
(25%) of the project area for outdoor space, plaza, pathways, and/or trails and open space.
      2.   Mixed Use: Unless otherwise approved through the MPD process, all Master Planned
Developments in the NMU-1 zone shall have a minimum of three (3) land uses (residential,
commercial, office, recreation, civic, etc.) integrated into the project area. A range of different uses
along the street, as well as diversity in building design and scale is encouraged wherever
practicable.
      3.   Limitation On Direct Retail Sales: Unless otherwise approved through the Master Planned
Development process, no single retail use may exceed fifty thousand (50,000) square feet.
      4.   Single-Story Commercial Building Standards: Unless otherwise approved through the
Master Planned Development process, no single-story commercial structures greater than one
thousand (1,000) square feet are permitted in the NMU-1 zone.
      5.   Access To Public Transportation: All Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone
shall provide facilities that substantially facilitate, enhance, and support the use of alternative public
transportation options such as transit services, neighborhood circulators, employee shuttles, car
share, bicycle share, and bicycle and pedestrian infrastructure. Unless otherwise permitted through
the Master Planned Development process, all Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone
shall provide a transit center, transportation stops and/or other improvements necessary to mitigate
the impacts of the development. Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned
Firefox https://export.amlegal.com/api/export-requests/c11155bf-f881-4cd5-93e...

EXHIBIT B.5
60
Development process, all transit centers shall include a building with a waiting area, restrooms,
bicycle lockers, and bike racks. All transportation stops shall include shelters and bicycle racks to
mitigate the impacts of the development.
      6.   Neighborhood Connectivity: All Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone shall
provide pedestrian and bicycle linkages within the project area as well as connections to
adjacent/off-site sidewalk, pathway, and trail systems. Wherever possible, the developer shall
separate pedestrian and bicycle linkages from vehicular areas without disrupting the pedestrian
way. Bicycle parking, storage, and bicycle racks consistent with the Summit County bicycle parking
standards, guidelines and regulations shall be provided.
      7.   The Public Realm: All Master Planned Developments in the NMU-1 zone shall provide a
project-specific public realm plan for all areas to which the public has access including but not
limited to streets, plazas, parking areas, sidewalks, pathways, and associated green spaces. The
design of all utilities, infrastructure, and signs/wayfinding shall be included with the plan.
      8.   Parking: The following parking requirements shall be met. When calculations of the number
of required off-street parking spaces result in a fractional number, any fraction of 0.5 or larger shall
be rounded up to the next higher whole number.
         a.   Residential Uses: Unless otherwise modified by the Master Planned Development
process, residential parking shall comply with Section 10-4-9 of this title.
         b.   Non-Residential Uses: The base parking ratio shall be three and one-half (3.5) off-street
parking spaces per each one thousand (1,000) square feet of nonresidential space.
      Exception: Parking greater than or less than three and one-half (3.5) off- street parking spaces
per one thousand (1,000) square feet of non-residential area may be permitted by the county
council as part of a Master Planned Development only after the applicant submits a parking study
for comparable uses which demonstrates that a higher/lower demand can be anticipated.
            (1)   When reviewing a request to exceed or reduce the overall parking requirement, the
county council shall consider:
               (A)   Structured parking;
               (B)   Traffic patterns in the area and how the proposed parking plan will affect them;
               (C)   Ingress and egress issues, and locations and whether they will affect traffic patterns;
               (D)   Requiring spaces for van pooling;
               (E)   Carpool spaces;
               (F)   Covered bike parking;
               (G)   Site location as it relates to transit;
               (H)   Shared parking;
               (I)   Other conclusions from the parking study; and
               (J)   The change in parking shall not result in adverse impacts to public walkways, plazas,
or other pedestrian circulation areas.
         c.   Parking Design: To minimize the potential adverse visual impacts of exterior parking, the
following requirements shall apply to all parking areas:

            (1)   Unless otherwise permitted through the Master Planned Development process, off-

thousand (10,000) square feet shall be permitted to design parking to the front facade provided
exterior parking lots, parking structures are hidden from view from most primary streets and
crossings serving the project and do not create unsafe pedestrian conditions.
            (2)   Parking structures shall be designed to have the appearance of horizontal storied
buildings that reflect the character of adjacent buildings.
            (3)   Storefronts shall be located on the street level and designed to create street scape,
shall be readily accessible by pedestrian/bicycle linkages and associated facilities.
            (4)   Structured parking design and space count shall consider project access, circulation,
convenience, and practicality and be required to conform to the parking guidelines in the NMU-1
zone. Design shall locate curb cuts away from the main thoroughfare.
            (5)   Developers shall utilize innovative parking reduction techniques such as shared
parking, structured parking, smaller stalls, car stackers and valet parking.
            (6)   Bicycle parking shall be provided. Bicycle parking shall be consistent with the county
bicycle parking standards, guidelines and regulations.
      9.   Architecture And Building Construction: The project architecture shall foster a distinct
neighborhood character. Designs shall be sensitive to the landscape and topography of the site.
While new structures are not required to mimic historic structures or themed designs, buildings in
the NMU-1 zone should reflect local and regional practices regarding materials, and roof forms.
Streetscapes should respond to the human, pedestrian scale rather than the automobile.
Architecture that relies on standardized corporate designs, mechanical climate control and
automobile accessibility is strongly discouraged.
      10.   Sustainabilitv: Projects shall be socially, economically, and environmentally sustainable.
Buildings should be designed, oriented, and constructed to maximize the potential for improved
efforts regarding:
         a.   Connectivity;
         b.   Energy efficiency;
         c.   On-site energy generation;
         d.   Solar orientation, natural light, and ventilation;
         e.   Thermal and light reduction;
         f.   Sustainable materials; and
         g.   Water conservation landscaping such as:
            (1)   Drought tolerant plant species;
            (2)   Native plant species;
            (3)   Xeriscaping;
            (4)   Time of day watering; and
            (5)   Rain or moisture sensors on irrigation systems.

      11.   Site Topography: The project shall be designed to incorporate existing site topography into

      12.   Affordable Housing: For purposes of the NMU-1 zone, and as a condition of receiving

EXHIBIT B.8
63
10-2-18: MASTER PLANNED DEVELOPMENTS:
   A.   Intent: A Master Planned Development (MPD) is a comprehensive project design strategy.
MPDs are intended to provide better opport
unities to create projects that address site conditions,
the characteristics of the surrounding properties, as well as community and market demands. The
MPD process creates administrative tools to promote the efficient use of land resources as well as
efficient public infrastructure and utility services. The goal of this strategy is to produce superior
project design through flexibility and innovation to advance the goals of the Snyderville Basin
General Plan and this code.
   B.   Applicability:
   Required: Unless the property is subject to an approved development agreement, settlement
agreement or consent agreement, the MPD process shall be required in all zones for the following:
      1.   Any application for a rezone;
      2.   A residential project with ten (10) or more units of density; and
      3.   Any new hotel or lodging project, commercial, retail, office, public, quasi-public, mixed use,
or industrial project with ten thousand (10,000) square feet or more of gross floor area.
   C.   Uses: An MPD can only contain uses, which are allowed, low impact or conditional in the
existing zone district(s) in which it is located. When the project area includes more than one (1)
zone district, uses may be relocated across zone district lines if the planning commission
determines that relocation results in a project design that advances the goals of the Snyderville
Basin General Plan.
   D.   Process:
      1.   Pre-Application Conference: A pre-application conference shall be held with staff for the
applicant to become acquainted with the MPD procedures and related county requirements and
schedules. Staff may give preliminary feedback to the applicant based on information available at
the conference and may inform the applicant of potential issues or special requirements which may
result from the proposal. Any direction or feedback given at the pre-application conference is not
vested. Vesting occurs once a complete application is filed, and the project has been reviewed and
approved by the county council or planning commission.
      2.   Pre-Application Work Session Public Meeting:
         a.   To provide an opportunity for the public and the planning commission to give preliminary
input on a concept for an MPD, the applicant may request a work session discussion with the
planning commission after the pre-application conference with staff. Any direction or feedback
given at the pre-application work session public meeting is not vested. Vesting occurs once a
complete application is filed, and the project has been reviewed and approved by the county
council or planning commission.
         b.   At the pre-application work session public meeting, the applicant will have an opportunity
to present the preliminary concepts for the proposed MPD. The public will be given an opportunity
to comment on the preliminary concepts so that the applicant can address neighborhood concerns
in preparation of an application for an MPD.
         c.   It is recommended that the applicant host neighborhood meetings in preparation of filing
of a formal application for an MPD and provide a report conveying neighborhood concerns
discussed.
Firefox https://export.amlegal.com/api/export-requests/d4ff7bee-f51a-41fe-be7...
1 of 9 1/8/2024, 11:46 AM
EXHIBIT C.1
64
      3.   The MPD Application:
         a.   Plans for the MPD shall be submitted with a completed application form supplied by the
county. A list of minimum requirements will accompany the application form. The application must
include written consent by all owners of the property to be included in the MPD. Once an
application is received, it shall be assigned to a staff planner who will review the application for
completeness. The applicant will be informed if additional information is necessary to constitute a
complete application.
         b.   Planning Commission Review And Public Hearing: Except in the case of MPDs that
include a rezone, the planning commission shall approve, approve with modifications, or deny an
MPD. The planning commission action shall be in the form of written findings of fact, conclusions of
law, and in the case of approval, conditions of approval. Action shall occur only after public hearing
is held. To approve an MPD, the planning commission will be required to make the findings outlined
in section F herein. In the case of MPDs that include a rezone, the planning commission shall
review and forward a recommendation to the county council, including findings of fact, conclusions
of law and conditions of approval.
         c.   County Council Review And Public Hearing: In the case of MPDs that include a rezone,
the county council shall approve, approve with modifications, or deny an MPD. The county council
action shall be in the form of written findings of fact, conclusions of law and in the case of approval,
conditions of approval. Action shall occur only after a public hearing is held. To approve an MPD,
the county council will be required to make the findings outlined in section F herein.
      4.   Development Agreement:
         a.   Once the planning commission or county council approves a Master Planned
Development, the approval shall be put in the form of a development agreement reviewed by the
county attorney's office and shall contain, at a minimum, the following:
            (1)   A legal description of the land;
            (2)   All relevant zoning and development code parameters, including all findings,
conclusions, and conditions of approval, specifying any exceptions outlining more or less restrictive
height or setbacks;
            (3)   An express reservation of the future legislative power and zoning authority of the
county;
            (4)   A copy of the approved site plan, architectural plans, landscaping plans, grading plan,
trails and open space plans, and other plans, which are a part of the approval;
            (5)   A description of all developer exactions or agreed upon public dedications;
            (6)   The developers agreement to pay all specified impact fees;
            (7)   The form of ownership anticipated for the project; and
            (8)   A specific project phasing plan.
         b.   The development agreement shall be signed by the chair of either the planning
commission or the chair of the county council and recorded with the Summit County recorder. The
development agreement shall contain language to allow for minor, administrative modifications
without revision of the agreement. The development agreement shall be reviewed and signed as
part of the final approval by the planning commission or county council.

EXHIBIT C.2
65
      5.   Vesting Of Approval:
         a.   MPDs Not Associated With A Final Subdivision Plat: Construction within the MPD area
will be required to commence within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval. After
construction commences, the MPD shall remain valid if it is consistent with the approved specific
project-phasing plan as set forth in the approved final site plan and associated documents. It is
anticipated that the specific project-phasing plan may require review and re-evaluation of the
project at specified points in the development of the project to be described in the development
agreement.
         b.   MPDs Not Associated With A Rezone But Requiring A Final Subdivision Plat: A final
subdivision plat must be recorded within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval.
Additionally, construction within the MPD area will be required to commence within five (5) years of
the date of the MPD approval. If the required final subdivision plat recordation and construction
commencement has not taken place in the prescribed timeframe, the MPD shall expire and the final
subdivision plat shall be void.
         c.   MPDs Associated With A Rezone, But Not Requiring A Final Subdivision Plat:
Construction within the MPD area will be required to commence within five (5) years of the date of
the MPD approval. After construction commences, the MPD shall remain valid if it is consistent with
the approved specific project-phasing plan as set forth in the approved final site plan and
associated documents. It is anticipated that the specific project-phasing plan may require review
and re-evaluation of the project at specified points in the development of the project. If the required
construction commencement has not taken place in the prescribed timeframe, the MPD shall expire
and the zone shall revert to the previous zone designation.
         d.   MPDs Associated With A Rezone And Final Subdivision Plat: Unless otherwise extended
per the provisions set forth in this chapter, a final subdivision plat associated with a rezone must be
recorded within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval. Additionally, construction within the
MPD area will be required to commence within five (5) years of the date of the MPD approval. If the
required final subdivision plat recordation and construction commencement has not taken place in
the prescribed timeframe, the MPD shall expire and the zone shall revert to the previous zone
designation.
      After recordation of the final subdivision plat and the commencement of construction, the MPD
shall remain valid if it is consistent with the approved specific project plan and associated
documents.
      6.   MPD Modifications:
         a.   Minor Amendment: A minor amendment is defined as an amendment that does not
increase square footage, density, or intensity of the previously approved MPD. A minor amendment
shall be processed as a low impact permit.
         b.   Major Amendment: A major amendment is defined as an amendment that increases
square footage, density, or intensity of the previously approved MPD. A major amendment shall be
processed as an MPD.
   E.   MPD Requirements: All MPD applications shall meet the following minimum requirements.
Additional project information necessary for the project analysis may be required at the discretion of
the community development director, planning commission, or county council.
      1.   Affected Properties: All MPD applications shall include all contiguous holdings by the owner,
unless specifically waived by the planning commission. The rezoning application shall be
accompanied by an affirmation of ownership signed by the legal owner of the property. If the
Firefox https://export.amlegal.com/api/export-requests/d4ff7bee-f51a-41fe-be7...
3 of 9 1/8/2024, 11:46 AM
EXHIBIT C.3
66
property is under contract for sale, the affirmation shall be signed by the contract owner and legal
owner and specify the date a contract of sale was executed. In the event corporations are involved,
a copy of the resolution legally empowering the applicant to make application shall be provided with
the application.
      2.   Density:
         a.   The type of development, number of units and density permitted on a given site will be
determined through a site suitability analysis and shall not exceed the maximum density in the
zone, except as otherwise provided in this section. The site shall be looked at in its entirety and the
density located in the most appropriate locations.
         b.   In cases where a project site contains more than one (1) zone district, the planning
commission may permit a shift of density between zone districts if the shift results in the project
advancing the goals set forth in the general plan.
         c.   Square footage associated with underground/structured parking areas within an MPD is
exempt from MPD density calculations.
         d.   Square footage associated with deed-restricted affordable housing units within an MPD is
exempt from MPD density calculations.
      3.   Setbacks: Setbacks for all structures within an MPD shall be determined by the
requirements found in each individual zone district.
         a.   Exception: To mitigate negative impacts, preserve view corridors or create a compatible
street design/streetscape, the planning commission or county council may modify the setbacks
around the exterior boundary of the project. In some cases, that setback may be increased to
create an adequate buffer to adjacent uses at the discretion of the county. The planning
commission/county council may reduce setbacks within the project from those otherwise required
provided the project meets minimum International Building Code and Fire Code requirements and
advances the goals set forth in the general plan.
      4.   Building Height: The maximum building height for all structures within an MPD shall not
exceed the requirements found in each individual zone district.
      5.   Site Planning: An MPD shall be designed to take into consideration characteristics of the
site upon which it is proposed to be placed. The project should be designed to fit the site, not the
site modified to fit the project. The following shall be addressed in the site planning for an MPD:
         a.   Designing With The Topography: MPDs shall be designed to fit into the topography of the
site. The planning commission/county council may consider flexibility in the siting of the
development to best fit into the natural terrain, minimize excessive site grading and the need for
excessive retaining.
      Efforts shall be made to mitigate impacts of the development on the natural environment and
resources of the surrounding area. The project design shall make suitable provisions for the
preservation of all affected critical lands.
         b.   Designing With Adjacent Uses: The MPD site plan shall take adjacent land uses into
consideration. Development along the project perimeter shall adequately mitigate any potentially
adverse influences, including but not limited to flooding, erosion, subsidence, sloping of the soil or
other dangers and nuisances.
         c.   Building Location: All buildings shall be located to avoid, to the extent practicable, critical
lands. Building locations and associated lot configurations should be oriented to encourage active
Firefox https://export.amlegal.com/api/export-requests/d4ff7bee-f51a-41fe-be7...
4 of 9 1/8/2024, 11:46 AM
EXHIBIT C.4
67
and passive solar design principles wherever practicable. Building design should create
recognizable streets and other spaces with their edges defined by buildings, making it easy for
anyone to find their way around, and promote safety and accessibility.
         d.   Access: All MPDs shall have vehicular access from a state highway or county road. All
developments shall have a secondary point of access/emergency access unless otherwise
mitigated to the satisfaction of the Summit County engineer and fire marshal. Roads, utility lines,
and buildings should be designed to work with the existing grade. Cuts and fills should be
minimized.
         e.   Trails: Existing trails should be incorporated into the project and should be maintained in
their existing location whenever possible. Trail easements for existing trails may be required.
Construction of new transportation and/or recreational trails will be required to be consistent with
Summit County's active transportation plan and/or Snyderville Basin special recreation district's
trails master plan, respectively. All new construction will meet or exceed the design standards set
forth in the plans.
         f.   Connectivity: All MPDs shall provide a means of direct, and safe pedestrian and bicycle
linkages within the project area as well as connections to adjacent/off-site sidewalk, pathway, and
trail systems. Pedestrian/ equestrian/bicycle circulation shall be separated from vehicular
circulation wherever reasonable.
      Where applicable, MPDs will have a system of streets, alleys, and pedestrian pathways with
multiple routes and connections serving the same origins and destinations. All streets, alleys, and
pedestrian pathways shall connect to other streets and to existing and projected streets outside the
proposed MPD or other development. Dead ends or cul-de-sacs are discouraged.
         g.   Snow Removal: Snow storage shall be compliant with the requirements of chapter
10-4-14 of this title. The site plan shall include adequate areas for snow removal and snow storage.
The landscape plan shall allow for snow storage areas. Structures shall be set back from any hard
surfaces to provide adequate areas to remove and store snow. Snow should be stored on site and
not removed to an off-site location.
         h.   Waste Disposal/Recycling Facilities: All waste disposal and recycling facilities shall be
compliant with the requirements of chapter 10-4-13 of this title. The site plan shall include adequate
areas for trash dumpsters and recycling containers, including an adequate circulation area for pick-
up vehicles. These facilities shall be enclosed and shall be included on the site and landscape
plans for the project. Pedestrian access shall be provided to the refuse/recycling facilities from
within the MPD for the convenience of residents and guests.
         i.   Service And Delivery Access: Service and delivery access and loading/unloading areas
must be included in the site plan. The service and delivery should be kept separate from pedestrian
areas.
         j.   Utilities: Existing or proposed utility and public services for MPDs will be adequate to
support the proposed project at normal service levels and will be designed in a manner to avoid
adverse impacts on adjacent land uses, public services, and utility resources, unless otherwise
permitted by this chapter.
      6.   Transportation: MPDs shall include transportation approaches that add more options for
public transportation, pedestrians, and bicycle users, and reduce the need for driving. An MPD
should include:
         a.   Transportation amenities including drop-off areas for van and shuttle service, and a bus
stop, if applicable;

         b.   Bike facilities including but not limited to lockers and racks;

      7.   Employee/Affordable Housing: All MPD applications shall include a housing mitigation plan

      8.   Open Space/Public Spaces: All MPDs shall provide at least the minimum zone-required

Community Commercial (CC) zones, open space areas shall include public space as defined in this
title. Public spaces shall:
         a.   Be well located to support a wide variety of activities and encourage social interaction,
that promote health, well-being, social and civic inclusion;
         b.   Have a hierarchy of spaces that range from large and strategic to small and local spaces,

chapter, all MPD shall comply with all requisite development evaluation standards found in chapter

      11.   General Plan Review: All MPD applications shall be reviewed for consistency with the
goals and objectives of the Snyderville Basin general plan; however, such review for consistency
shall not alone be binding.

new natural features into a multifunctional network that supports quality of place, biodiversity, and
water management? How does it address climate change mitigation and resilience? Will
landscaping work to achieve this goal?
         b.   Water Quality: What measures are being taken to manage water use by the development,
and what is being done to ensure the development does not contaminate ground water or
surrounding water ways.
         c.   Air Quality: Explain what measures are being taken to ensure the development does not
have a negative effect on Summit County's air quality.
         d.   Protection Of Critical Lands: If the development is adjacent to critical lands, or within a

mix of housing types and affordability?
         d.   Character: What are the design objectives for the built environment, including buildings

   F.   Required Findings Of Fact: The county must find enough evidence to support the following
conclusions to approve an MPD. In some cases, conditions of approval will be attached to the
approval to ensure compliance with these findings.
      1.   The MPD is designed to fit well into the natural terrain, minimize excessive site grading and
protect, preserve, and enhance the level of quality of the surrounding area.
      2.   The MPD makes suitable provisions for the protection, preservation, and enhancement of
watercourses, drainage areas, wooded areas, rough terrain and similar natural features and areas.
      3.   The MPD takes adjacent land uses into consideration and mitigates potential impacts,
including but not limited to flooding, erosion, subsidence, sloping of the soil or other dangers and
nuisances, through careful planning.
      4.   The MPD has direct vehicular access from a state highway or county road or suitable
private road or driveway access meeting all requirements of the county engineer and fire marshal.
      5.   The MPD has a secondary point of access/emergency access or other mitigation
satisfactory to the Summit County engineer and fire marshal.
      6.   All roads/streets within MPD follow the natural contours of the site wherever possible to
minimize the amount of grading.
      7.   Existing or proposed utility and public services are adequate to support the proposed MPD
at normal service levels and are designed in a manner to avoid adverse impacts on adjacent land
uses, public services, and utility resources.
      8.   The proposed structures within the MPD are located on the most developable and least
environmentally sensitive portions of the site. The open areas within the MPD are designed so that

From:   Tiffanie N. Robinson, Senior Planner

Type of Item:  Canyons Village Parking Garage - Low Impact Permit 
Continued Discussion, Possible Recommendation  
Process:  Administrative Review 
 
 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY:  
 
Summit County Planning Department has received a Low Impact Permit request for the 
construction of the Canyons Village Parking Garage  to be located at 1840 Ozzy Way, Lower 
Village Development Area. Staff recommends that the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission 
review the Canyons Village Parking Garage Low Impact Permit , consider Staff’s analysis and the 
CVMA recommendation  and forward a positive recommendatio n to t he Community 
Development Director , based on the findings of fact, conclusion of law and conditions of 
approval, outlined in the staff report.   
 
Project Description 
 
Project Name: Canyons Village Parking Garage 
Low Impact Permit

representatives

Final Land Use Authority: Community Development Director  
 
Proposal 
 
The applicants, representing TCFC GarageCo, LP , are requesting a Low Impact Permit  approval 
for the Canyons Village Parking Garage , located at 1840 Ozzy Way in the Lower Village 
Development Areat at Canyons Village.  The proposed parking structure  and contiguous  
 
76
Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Cont. Discussion, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 26, 2024 
surface parking will be located on parcel LV5 and will provide 
1,840 parking stalls  EXHIBIT A. The entire structure is 5 -levels, 
however, d ue to the existing topography, sloping 
approximately 26’ feet from north to south, the southern end 
of the structure but only be 3 -levels above grade , while the 
north end will be 5-levels above grade and will walk out directly 
to the Cabriolet plaza level. 
 
The Canyons Village Parking Garage  will be a multi- year 
construction project.  The applicant has provided an outline  of 
the anticipated phasing EXHIBIT B. 
 
• Phase 1 includes planning approvals, permitting, s ite work and construction of the first 
three (3) levels of the parking structure.  This phase is intended to create two parking 
levels that would be operational for the 2024/2025 ski seasons.  The third floor would 
act as the parking structure roof.  There will be a temporary surface parking area  on the 
adjacent lot, LVDAM-1B-2-AM, to accommodate summer resort activities. 
• Phase 2 includes completion of the remaining two  (2) floors, all architectural finishes 
and final landscaping installation. The garage will be complete and fully operational by 
December 2025 for the 2025/2026 ski season.

Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Cont. Discussion, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 26, 2024 
Background 
 
The Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement anticipated a parking  structure 
and/or parking lot  to be located on parcels LV1B and LV5.  The int ent is to provide day skier 
parking adjacent to the Cabriolet and capture vehicles at the entrance of the Resort, reducing 
traffic on Canyons Resort Drive and into the Resort Core.    
 
 
On November 16,  2023, an application was received for a Low Impact Permit  for approval, as 
required by the Development Agreement for the proposed parking structure  for LV5 . The 
application was unable to move forward at that time because the LV5 parcel had not been 
platted, entitling the use.   
 
On November 16, 2023, an administrative amendment  application was received to modify  
the Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement requesting a modification to 
the maximum height allowed for the LV5 development parcel.  Per the 2018 Amended and 
Restated Canyons Development Agreement,  the  allowed height for LV5 is  a maximum of 
6,780’ ASL.  There are some limited exceptions that are allowed to exceed the height 
standard such as antennas, chimney flus, venting, elevator penthouses, mechanical 
equipment, and rooftop equipment for “green Initiatives”. During the design process  for the 
garage, the upper level did not roof  cap.  However, after considering inclusion of a roof the 
applicants state “ taking into account a ﬂat roof structure, and the use of tapered insulation to 
provide slope for drainage, it would be feasible to construct a roof over the garage with an 
additional 3' of height though 4' would allow for more aggressive drainage slopes.  It has been 
investigated to lower the garage in lieu of exploring the Amendment for additional height. This 
option would result in essentially pushing the garage down into a hole that would necessitate 
the employment of stairs and ramps to access the cabriolet and associated plaza. The current 
design allows patrons to exit out from the garage at grade, engaging with the plaza. ”  
Therefore, the application was made to request the amendment. 
 
On January 9, 2024, a Plat Amendment and Final Subdivision Plat application was submitted to 
create the LV5 development parcel.   
 
On February 27, 2024, the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission recommended approval of 
the Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amendment and LV1B and LV5 
Subdivision.  On March 5, 2024, the Summit County Manger conducted a public hearing and 
approved the Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amendment and LV1B and 
LV5 Subdivision.  
 
On March 6, 2024, Community Development Director, Pat Putt, formally approved the 
Administrative Amendment for the Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement 
Land Use and Zoning Table for LV5.   The approval amends the maximum height for LV5 from 
6,780’ ASL to be a maximum height 6,784’ ASL, for the garage only EXHIBIT C.  
78
Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Cont. Discussion, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 26, 2024

Analysis and Findings 
Se
ction 10-3-4(E) of the Snyderville Basin Development Code requires that any use qualifying 
for a Low Impact Permit must meet the following criteria: 
C
riteria 1 : The use does not significantly increase vehicular traffic, unless the increases are 
consistent with previously approved plans for which appropriate mitigation has been 
contemplated and which has been implemented in an appropriate manner to accommodate 
the proposed amendment.  
An
alysis: The proposed Canyons Village Parking Garage  will not significantly increase 
vehicular traffic at Canyons Village. The garage is intended to capture existing traffic 
that would otherwise be driving up Canyons Resort Drive and into the Resort Core. 
Ongoing transportation options and mitigation are required as part of the current 
Canyons Transportation plan and Transit Agreement. The parking structure is part of the 
mitigation plan  and is an obligation to be satisfied . The most recent com prehensive 
Canyons Traffic Study was completed in 2022.  The purpose of the updated TCFC Parking 
Structure traffic s tudy is to analyze traffic operations at key intersections for full- build 
conditions associated with the proposed Canyons Parking Garage  and to recommend 
mitigation measures, as needed EXHIBIT D1.   
At
 the March 12, 2024, hearing, the commission requested input from Summit County 
transportation s taff including their opinion or validation of the assumptions and 
outcomes, an overall opinion  of the study results, and their thoughts on the efficacy of 
the mitigations in the report. After reviewing the mo st recent updates to the traffic 
study, Brandon Brady , Summit County Transportation Planning Deputy Director, 
79
Canyons Village Parking Garage LIP- Cont. Discussion, Possible Recommendation - #23-191 – March 26, 2024 
provided a memorandum addressing the a ssumptions, analy sis and  recommended 
mitigation efforts EXHIBIT D2. 
C
riteria 2: The use does not significantly increase the demand for parking; unless the increases 
are consistent with previously approved plans for which appropriate mitigation has been 
contemplated and which has been implemented in an appropriate manner to accommodate 
the proposed amendment.  
An
alysis: The proposed Canyons Village Parking  Garage/Parking Area has been 
anticipated since the original 1998 DA was approved.  The garage will not significantly 
increase the demand for parking in Canyons Village.  There are currently 2,093 stalls 
within the Resort Core and the Lower Village area.  Except for  the existing Pendry hotel 
133 underground  parking spaces, 10 surface spaces at the Medical Clinic and the Vail 
Team building  (Lower Village)  108 surface parking spaces, all other surface parking in 
the Resort Core, RC16, RC21 and RC22 , will be relocated into the parking garage . 
Updated graphic information regarding the parking reconciliation  is demonstra ted in 
EXHIBIT D3. 
C
riteria 3: The use does not intensify the likelihood of pedestrian and vehicular conflicts. 
An
alysis: The applicant has considered traffic flow patterns for ingress and egress to try 
and limit the potential conflicts with pedestrians and vehicles.  A conscious decision was 
made not  include a sidewalk along Ozzy Way , directly adjacent to the surface parking 
and the two entry points to the garage , to try and minimize the interaction between 
pedestrians and vehicles.  The egress from the parking structure will be limited to a right 
turn only.  The existing crosswalk from the golf maintenance and  Vail team building will 
be relocated on the south side of this  egress to try and minimize pedestrian conflicts for 
those crossing from the west side of Lower Village Road.  The egress will cross Millenium 
Trail.  No vertical vegetation has been located along this area in order to prevent 
potential site line impairment for pedestrians or vehicle operators .  Supplemental 
information regarding signage, striping, stamped con crete a nd other safety measures 
have been provided in EXHIBIT D4. 
C
riteria 4 : The use does not create unsightly conditions, including, but not limited to, 
unscreened storage and other environmental concerns.  
An
alysis: Staff does not anticipate unsightly conditions or environmental concerns.

Findings of Fact: 
 
1. TCFC GarageCo, LP  is the fee title owner of parcels PP-102-C-2-C, PP-102-B-12, PP-102-
B-3-A, PP-102-B-3 and PP-PW-1-610-A, creating lot LVDAM-LV5-AM. 
2. The Canyons Amended and Restated Development Agreement allocated LV5 with 
parking/parking garage use that could be a maximum of 6780’ ASL. 
3. On November 16, 2023, a Low Impact Permit was submitted for the proposed Canyons 
Village Parking Garage.   
4. On November 16, 2023, an Administrative Amendment to the Canyons Amended and 
Restated Development Agreement to allow for an additional four (4’) of height for the 
parking structure was submitted to the Community Development Director. 
5. On January 9 , 2024, a Plat Amendment and Final Subdivision Plat application was 
submitted to create the LV5 development parcel. 
6. On February 27, 2024, the Snyderville Basin Planning Commission recommended 
approval of the Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amendment and 
LV1B and LV5 Subdivision.  On March 5, 2024, the Summit County Manger conducted a 
public hearing and approved the Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat 
Amendment and LV1B and LV5 Subdivision.  
7. On March 6, 2024, Community Development Director, Pat Putt, formally approved the 
Administrative Amendment for the Canyons Amended and Restated Development 
Agreement Land Use and Zoning Table for LV5.   The approval amends the maximum 
height for LV5 from 6,780’ ASL to be a maximum height 6,784’ ASL, for the garage only. 
8. The Snyderville Basin Development Code Section 10 -3-4(C.2) states; In proposals where 
the CDD or designated planning staff member determines that potential issues may arise 
or additional comment is needed from the community, a public hearing on the 
application may be scheduled with the planning commission. The applicant shall receive 
notice of the hearing. Following the public hearing, the planning commission shall make 
a recommendation to the CDD or designated planning staff member regarding an 
approval, approval with conditions or denial of the application. 
9. Staff has scheduled the item before the Planning Commission as a public hearing to 
provide the opportunity for public input from the community. 
10. Postcard notices announcing the public hearing were mailed to property owners within 
1,000 feet of the subject parcels. 
11. Public notice of the public hearing was published in The Park Record on March 2, 2024. 
12. Staff has reviewed the proposal for compliance with applicable Development Code 
standards. 
13. Service providers have reviewed the proposal for compliance with applicable standards. 
14. The parking structure is in compliance with Canyons Amended and Restated 
Development Agreement and is a vital piece of the Canyons Transportation Plan  and 
ongoing transit mitigation efforts. 
15. The project is consistent with the Global Principals outlined in the Canyons 
Development Agreement.  
 
Conclusions of Law:

1. The proposed project, as conditioned, meets the requisite standards set forth in the 
Snyderville Basin Development Code, Section 10-3-4: Low Impact Permit. 
 
Conditions of Approval: 
 
1. Construction of the project shall follow the approved plans submitted to the 
Community Development Department.  Changes to the plans are subject to an 
amendment of the Low Impact Permit. 
2. No modification to this Low Impact Permit is permitted without the prior approval of 
the Summit County Community Development Department.   
3. All lighting and landscaping details and compliance verification must be reviewed 
and approved by the Summit County Planning prior to issuance of the garage 
building permit.  
4. A Construction Mitigation, including a detailed  phasing and  staging plan shall be 
finalized prior to issuance of a development  permit.  This should include a detailed 
re-vegetation and re-establishment plan for all disturbed areas that are not included 
in the landscaping plan. A bond in the amount of which includes erosion control , re-
vegetation, re -establishment of temporary parking area and staging areas and 
landscaping must be submitted to the County Engineer, prior to any development. 
5. Stormwater Pollution and Prevention Plan and Erosion Control Plan shall be 
followed, and all necessary bonding shall be submitted to Summit County prior to 
any development activity associated with the project.   
6. A current Notice of Intent must be filed and provided to the County Engineer, prior 
to commencement of development.   
7. All conditions of the Canyons DRC must be met. 
8. All requirements of the applicable service providers shall be followed.  
9. All necessary permits must be obtained prior to the commencement of any 
construction activity including, but not limited to , building permits and engineering 
permits.   
10. The Lower Village Master Development Area Master Plat Amendment and L V1B and 
LV5 Subdivision must be recorded prior to any development activity on site. 
11. All existing surface parking areas within the Resort Core on development parcels 
RC16B, RC21 and RC22 shall  no longer be utilized for parking, upon receipt of 
certificate of occupancy for the entire parking garage. 
12. A certificate of elevation must be submitted to verify compliance with the maximum 
allowed height. 
13. The applicant and garage operator shall continue to work with the Transportation 
Department and High Valley Transit to discuss mitigation strategies, operations and 
transit routing for the garage and transportation improvements in future phases. 
14. A pedestrian connection shall maintained to provide direct access from the Cabriolet 
Plaza to the northwest corner of the lot, to comply with the Canyons connectivity 
master plan. 
15. The applicant shall implement traffic mitigation strategies  consistent with 
recommendations from the TCFC Parking Structure Hales Traffic Study , dated March 
12, 2024, and County Transportation Department.

The following describes the parking garage to be designed and built in Canyons Village adjacent to the bottom 
terminal of the cabriolet lift.  Overall, it is expected the project will deliver an efficient, effective, and safe parking 
facility that results in a suitable visitor experience as allowed by the site, budget, and schedule.   
The project will consist of ﬁve levels of parking with a roof (if the SPA administrative amendment is approved).  
There is an integrated guest drop off zone on the north end of level 2 as well as vehicular access points on levels 2 
and 3.  Pedestrian access to the cabriolet and adjacent Transit Center is via the plaza on Level 1.  Vertical circulation 
is provided by 6 full-height stair towers, 2 elevators, and a bank of escalators on the east side that run from Level 5 
the Level 1.  The stairs and elevators are fully enclosed while the escalators are protected from the elements but 
open to the interior.  
The structure is II-B construction using long-span concrete beams and is naturally ventilated per IBC requirements.   
The facade has extensive aesthetic treatments beﬁtting its location adjacent to the entry of the resort. 
The project is proposing a low slope roof structure with vertical circulation elements extending above said low 
slope roof for articulation of the design.  Though in slight excess of the prescribed height, the beneﬁts to the 
community are: 
• Compliance with Dark Skies while ensuring proper lighting for guest safety. 
• The elimination of the need to snow melt level 5. 
• Shielding the parking from those uphill from the site. 
• Improved safety due to reduced potential for slip and falls.

Memorandum:
The parking garage project is planned to take place in two phases.  Per discussions with Summit 
County these two phases will now be formally submitted in two unique bid sets for permitting.  
At a high level the phases break down as follows:
Phase I - March to November, 2024.  Work to be included; mass excavation, shoring wall 
construction, site grading, site utilities, construction of levels 1-3 of the parking garage.
Phase II - March to November 2025.  Work to be included; construction of levels 4-5 and 
the roof of the garage, all facade materials, elevated drop off lane and mass timber roof 
structure, completion of all vertical circulation including completion of stairs and 
erection of escalators and elevators, associated enclosures, all surface paving and 
pedestrian circulation.  
Further delineation of the two scopes of work can be found on the attached exiting plans, ﬂoor 
plans, site plans and elevations for Phase I and II, attached.
Please ﬁnd following responses to the speciﬁc follow up questions included in the February 29, 
2024 email;
1. When do you anticipated commencement of site grading?  Response: Wadsworth Brothers is
anticipating pulling a construction permit by March 28, 2024 at which time excavation will
commence.
2. You mentioned that you will be moving 40,000 yards.  Where will this be going?  Based on the cut/ﬁll
analysis, not much will remain onsite.  Will it be used in the resort?  Response: A Browns Canyon pit
X Email FTP Hand Mail Other
Recipient Company Email/Address
Tiffanie Robinson Summit County trobinson@summitcounty.org
Laurel Simpson TCFC lsimpson@tc-fc.com
Spencer White white.spencer@gmail.com
Mark Oakeson Wadsworth Brothers moakeson@wadsbro.com

EXHIBIT B1
126
located off of Browns Canyon Road is the intended destination for the spoils generated from the 
mass excavation.
3. When do you anticipate going vertical?  Just clarifying, you indicated in your response that you will 
obtain two (2) separate building permits, correct?  Response: It is anticipated the project would “go 
vertical” about April 29.   Wadsworth Brothers would initially pull the previously discussed 
construction permit to allow for excavation to commence while the building permit review process 
is being completed.  Then a Phase I Building Permit would be obtained for the ’24 construction 
season and a subsequent permit would be pulled for the Phase II construction (the balance of the 
work) to be conducted and completed in the ’25 construction season.  
4. When will all architectural elements be completed?   Response: All architectural elements would be 
completed by the 25/26 ski season.
5. Landscaping (will this be phased?) [sic}   Response: Landscaping would be completed as part of the 
Phase II package. 
6. Sidewalk/circulation improvements (explain temporary and permanent)  - FYI, this will be a big 
discussion point for the SBPC given this was a primary concern in 2017 for the Employee Housing 
access.  Response: The project would maintain pedestrian circulation on and around the site 
differently depending upon the time of year.  During the construction season public access would 
be stopped across the site.  Outside of these two construction seasons public access around and 
through the site would be maintained.  Please refer to the attached pedestrian circulation and 
construction phasing plans.
7. Temporary laydown area – this is new to me. When will it be utilized (summer of 2024 or not until ski 
seasons)?  When will it be removed and reestablished? Lighting? Bonding?  Response: The laydown 
areas shown will be used during Phase I construction and then reestablished after Phase I is 
completed for use during the 2024-2025 ski season.  All space designated as laydown will be 
occupied from about April 15, 2024 through November 30, 2024 and then will be reestablished with 
the same parking surface materials as are currently there.  
8. Anything else you think is important to include that I may have missed. Response: No other items 
come to mind at this time.  If something does come up while writing your report please do not 
hesitate to call me at 801-971-8260 (cell).

Landscape Narrative:
The landscape plan for the TCFC Parking Structure will comply with the SPA Design
Guidelines for the Lower Village at the Canyons Village at the Park City Resort.  The
plants specified In the plan will be native to the high mountain climate of the resort.
They will be sensitive to creating a natural looking landscape that can be enjoyed by
both people living in the development and passing by.  A landscape buffer will be
created between the structure and the existing residential areas.  Landscape material
will also be proposed along Canyons Resort Drive.
